### Mask Image Watermarking
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.12739v1)
- **Authors**: Runyi Hu, Jie Zhang, Shiqian Zhao, Nils Lukas, Jiwei Li, Qing Guo, Han Qiu, Tianwei Zhang
- **Abstract**: We present MaskMark, a simple, efficient and flexible framework for image watermarking. MaskMark has two variants: MaskMark-D, which supports global watermark embedding, watermark localization, and local watermark extraction for applications such as tamper detection, and MaskMark-ED, which focuses on local watermark embedding and extraction with enhanced robustness in small regions, enabling localized image protection. Built upon the classical Encoder- Distortion-Decoder training paradigm, MaskMark-D introduces a simple masking mechanism during the decoding stage to support both global and local watermark extraction. A mask is applied to the watermarked image before extraction, allowing the decoder to focus on selected regions and learn local extraction. A localization module is also integrated into the decoder to identify watermark regions during inference, reducing interference from irrelevant content and improving accuracy. MaskMark-ED extends this design by incorporating the mask into the encoding stage as well, guiding the encoder to embed the watermark in designated local regions for enhanced robustness. Comprehensive experiments show that MaskMark achieves state-of-the-art performance in global watermark extraction, local watermark extraction, watermark localization, and multi-watermark embedding. It outperforms all existing baselines, including the recent leading model WAM for local watermarking, while preserving high visual quality of the watermarked images. MaskMark is also flexible, by adjusting the distortion layer, it can adapt to different robustness requirements with just a few steps of fine-tuning. Moreover, our approach is efficient and easy to optimize, requiring only 20 hours on a single A6000 GPU with just 1/15 the computational cost of WAM.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MaskMark, a new image watermarking framework designed for both global and local watermark embedding and extraction.  It offers two variants: MaskMark-D (global embedding with local extraction) and MaskMark-ED (local embedding and extraction).  The core innovation lies in a masking mechanism during the decoding (and encoding, for MaskMark-ED) phase, allowing the model to focus on specific regions for watermark extraction or embed watermarks in those designated regions. The framework also integrates a localization module to identify watermarked areas. Experimental results demonstrate that MaskMark achieves state-of-the-art performance in global and local watermark extraction, watermark localization, and multi-watermark embedding while maintaining high visual quality and requiring lower computational resources compared to existing methods like WAM.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of incorporating a masking mechanism, particularly within the decoding/encoding stages, is a worthwhile contribution.  The masking allows the model to concentrate on specific regions, improving local watermark extraction and providing greater flexibility in application. The integrated localization module to identify the watermarked regions, and the application of the mask during training are also novel aspects.  While the overall framework builds on the encoder-distortion-decoder paradigm, the specific combination of masking and localization is novel.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in addressing the limitations of traditional global watermarking methods by enabling localized watermarking. This has important implications for applications like tamper detection, fine-grained forensic analysis, and targeted content protection. The reported efficiency gains (15x less compute than WAM) are also significant, making the approach more accessible. The ability to scale to higher bit lengths is a real strength. A drawback is that the gains in computational efficiency are only contrasted against WAM. It is not contrasted against other global watermark methods like TrustMark. Moreover, the paper does not adequately compare the advantages and disadvantages of applying the masking in different ways to the MaskMark-D or MaskMark-ED. Why is the MaskMark-D appropriate in some cases and the MaskMark-ED better in others?

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Strong performance in global and local watermarking tasks.
    *   Effective watermark localization.
    *   Scalability to higher bit lengths.
    *   Computational efficiency compared to WAM.
    *   Flexibility via fine-tuning for adaptive attacks.
    *   Good experimental validation with various distortions.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The comparison for computational efficiency is limited primarily to WAM. A broader comparison would strengthen the claims.
    *   The method is only compared to a limited number of watermarking methods, especially in watermark localization.
    *   The paper needs to discuss when it's more beneficial to use the MaskMark-D and when it's more beneficial to use the MaskMark-ED.
    *   Ablation studies on the importance of each of the parts of the model. What parts of the model provide the greatest performance? Are there parts of the model that aren't needed?

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by providing a more practical and flexible solution for image watermarking. The efficiency and scalability could make it more widely adopted. The idea of incorporating a mask in the encoder and decoder may inspire novel approaches to watermarking. However, its influence is dependent on the adoption of the method.

**Justification:**

While the core idea of masking during decoding/encoding is relatively simple, it has a significant effect on performance and flexibility. The paper adequately demonstrates the benefits of this approach across a range of tasks and scenarios. The computational efficiency is a real plus. However, a more robust comparison with other leading watermarking methods would be great.

### Privacy Protection Against Personalized Text-to-Image Synthesis via Cross-image Consistency Constraints
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.12747v1)
- **Authors**: Guanyu Wang, Kailong Wang, Yihao Huang, Mingyi Zhou, Zhang Qing cnwatcher, Geguang Pu, Li Li
- **Abstract**: The rapid advancement of diffusion models and personalization techniques has made it possible to recreate individual portraits from just a few publicly available images. While such capabilities empower various creative applications, they also introduce serious privacy concerns, as adversaries can exploit them to generate highly realistic impersonations. To counter these threats, anti-personalization methods have been proposed, which add adversarial perturbations to published images to disrupt the training of personalization models. However, existing approaches largely overlook the intrinsic multi-image nature of personalization and instead adopt a naive strategy of applying perturbations independently, as commonly done in single-image settings. This neglects the opportunity to leverage inter-image relationships for stronger privacy protection. Therefore, we advocate for a group-level perspective on privacy protection against personalization. Specifically, we introduce Cross-image Anti-Personalization (CAP), a novel framework that enhances resistance to personalization by enforcing style consistency across perturbed images. Furthermore, we develop a dynamic ratio adjustment strategy that adaptively balances the impact of the consistency loss throughout the attack iterations. Extensive experiments on the classical CelebHQ and VGGFace2 benchmarks show that CAP substantially improves existing methods.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the privacy concerns arising from the advancements in personalized text-to-image synthesis, where models can recreate individual portraits from a small number of publicly available images. The authors argue that existing anti-personalization methods, which add adversarial perturbations to images to disrupt the training of personalization models, are limited because they treat each image independently. The paper proposes a novel framework called Cross-image Anti-Personalization (CAP) that enhances resistance to personalization by enforcing style consistency across the perturbed images. CAP includes a style consistency loss that encourages similar style representations across images and a dynamic ratio adjustment strategy to balance the impact of the consistency loss during the attack. The authors present experimental results on CelebA-HQ and VGGFace2 datasets, demonstrating the effectiveness of CAP in improving privacy protection.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The core novelty lies in reframing anti-personalization as a cross-image consistency problem.  Existing methods largely treated each image in isolation, neglecting the fact that personalization models learn from multiple images of the same subject. The idea of explicitly modeling inter-image relationships, particularly through style consistency, is a valuable contribution. The dynamic ratio adjustment strategy, while not groundbreaking in itself, adds another layer of refinement to the attack process.

**Significance:** The paper's significance stems from the growing threat of unauthorized identity cloning facilitated by personalized generative models.  Addressing privacy concerns in this context is critical for responsible AI development. The empirical results, demonstrating improvements over strong baselines like Anti-DreamBooth and SimAC, add weight to the paper's claims.

**Strengths:**

*   **Problem Framing:** Identifying the limitations of existing image-wise anti-personalization methods and reframing the problem as a cross-image consistency challenge is insightful.
*   **Technical Approach:** The introduction of the style consistency loss, leveraging Gram matrices, is a well-motivated way to encourage similar style representations across images.
*   **Empirical Validation:** Thorough experiments on standard datasets and against strong baselines provide strong evidence of CAP's effectiveness. The ablation studies further isolate the contributions of different components of the framework.
*   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and organized, making it easy to understand the problem, approach, and experimental results.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Style Representation:** The paper relies on Gram matrices, a relatively simple style representation. More sophisticated style transfer methods could potentially offer further improvements.
*   **Computational Cost:** While the paper does not extensively discuss computational overhead, introducing a style consistency loss and dynamic ratio adjustment likely increases the computational cost of generating adversarial perturbations.
*   **Generalizability:** The experiments are limited to facial images. It's unclear how well the approach would generalize to other types of images or objects.
*   **Incremental Improvement:** The improvement over SimAC, while present, is perhaps not as substantial as one might hope given the additional complexity of CAP.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a solid contribution to the field of privacy protection in generative AI. The core idea of cross-image consistency is novel and well-motivated, the technical approach is sound, and the empirical results provide strong evidence of effectiveness. While the improvements over state-of-the-art are somewhat incremental, and there is room for further exploration of style representations and generalizability, the paper makes a valuable contribution to a rapidly evolving field.

### Trajectory Adaptation using Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.12755v1)
- **Authors**: Anurag Maurya, Tashmoy Ghosh, Ravi Prakash
- **Abstract**: Adapting robot trajectories based on human instructions as per new situations is essential for achieving more intuitive and scalable human-robot interactions. This work proposes a flexible language-based framework to adapt generic robotic trajectories produced by off-the-shelf motion planners like RRT, A-star, etc, or learned from human demonstrations. We utilize pre-trained LLMs to adapt trajectory waypoints by generating code as a policy for dense robot manipulation, enabling more complex and flexible instructions than current methods. This approach allows us to incorporate a broader range of commands, including numerical inputs. Compared to state-of-the-art feature-based sequence-to-sequence models which require training, our method does not require task-specific training and offers greater interpretability and more effective feedback mechanisms. We validate our approach through simulation experiments on the robotic manipulator, aerial vehicle, and ground robot in the Pybullet and Gazebo simulation environments, demonstrating that LLMs can successfully adapt trajectories to complex human instructions.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a language-based framework for adapting robot trajectories using large language models (LLMs). It utilizes pre-trained LLMs to interpret human instructions and environmental context, generating Python code as a policy for dense robot manipulation. This approach allows for more complex instructions, including numerical inputs, compared to existing methods. The generated code adapts generic robotic trajectories produced by motion planners (RRT, A*) or learned from demonstrations. The framework is evaluated through simulation experiments involving manipulators, aerial vehicles, and ground robots, demonstrating the LLM's capability to adapt trajectories based on complex human instructions. The framework emphasizes a user feedback loop to refine trajectory adaptations.  A key claim is that this approach bypasses the need for task-specific training and offers interpretability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty lies primarily in the *combination* of several existing ideas rather than a breakthrough innovation. Using LLMs for robotics is not new, nor is code generation for robot control or trajectory adaptation. The authors are putting them all together in a pipeline with a feedback loop. The core idea of using code as an intermediate representation between natural language commands and robot actions is a valuable contribution, enhancing both interpretability and editability. The idea of incorporating numerical parameters into the adaptation is also a step forward.

*   **Significance:** The significance is moderate. The framework simplifies the process of trajectory adaptation, especially for non-expert users. It could potentially lead to more intuitive human-robot interaction. The absence of task-specific training is a notable advantage, contributing to scalability. However, the evaluation remains confined to simulation. A real-world demonstration is needed to truly assess robustness and practical applicability. The paper does not provide rigorous quantitative comparisons against existing trajectory adaptation methods. This is a major drawback. The reliance on GPT-4 also raises concerns about cost and availability in resource-constrained settings.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Flexibility:** The framework's ability to handle complex instructions with numerical inputs is a significant strength.
    *   **Interpretability:** Code generation promotes transparency, allowing users to understand and modify the adaptation logic.
    *   **No Task-Specific Training:** This makes the approach more scalable and adaptable to new environments and tasks.
    *   **Feedback Loop:** The iterative feedback mechanism enhances accuracy and addresses misinterpretations.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Evaluation:** The evaluation is primarily in simulation, lacking real-world validation.
    *   **Lack of Quantitative Comparison:** The paper doesn't offer a performance comparison against other state-of-the-art trajectory adaptation methods.
    *   **LLM Dependency:** The dependency on powerful LLMs like GPT-4 raises concerns about computational costs and real-time performance.
    *   **No formal guarantees:** The paper acknowledges the lack of formal guarantees which is a critical point.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence research in human-robot interaction and trajectory planning, particularly in scenarios requiring intuitive adaptation based on natural language. However, realizing this potential depends on addressing the identified weaknesses, specifically through real-world validation and quantitative comparisons.

*   **Rigor of Rationale:** Given the relatively low level of novelty, and limited real-world evaluation, a higher score would not be justifiable. A lot of claims like 'intuitive interface' or 'scalability' are based on assumptions and can only be truly evaluated in user studies or real world applications.

**Score: 5**

**Rationale:** While the paper presents a promising approach to language-based trajectory adaptation, it falls short of making a significant breakthrough. The novelty is incremental, combining existing techniques in a new way. The lack of real-world validation and quantitative comparisons limits the demonstrated impact. The weaknesses outweigh the strengths in terms of a truly novel and impactful contribution.


- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 5/10

### GraphOmni: A Comprehensive and Extendable Benchmark Framework for Large Language Models on Graph-theoretic Tasks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.12764v1)
- **Authors**: Hao Xu, Xiangru Jian, Xinjian Zhao, Wei Pang, Chao Zhang, Suyuchen Wang, Qixin Zhang, Joao Monteiro, Qiuzhuang Sun, Tianshu Yu
- **Abstract**: In this paper, we presented GraphOmni, a comprehensive benchmark framework for systematically evaluating the graph reasoning capabilities of LLMs. By analyzing critical dimensions, including graph types, serialization formats, and prompt schemes, we provided extensive insights into the strengths and limitations of current LLMs. Our empirical findings emphasize that no single serialization or prompting strategy consistently outperforms others. Motivated by these insights, we propose a reinforcement learning-based approach that dynamically selects the best serialization-prompt pairings, resulting in significant accuracy improvements. GraphOmni's modular and extensible design establishes a robust foundation for future research, facilitating advancements toward general-purpose graph reasoning models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "GRAPHOMNI: A Comprehensive and Extendable Benchmark Framework for Large Language Models on Graph-theoretic Tasks":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces GRAPHOMNI, a new benchmark designed to comprehensively evaluate the reasoning capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) on graph-theoretic tasks. The benchmark focuses on assessing LLMs' performance across various graph types, serialization formats, and prompting schemes. Through extensive experiments, the authors identify critical interactions between these dimensions and their impact on model performance.  They find that even state-of-the-art models have considerable room for improvement, with performance varying significantly based on the specific combination of graph types, serialization formats, and prompting strategies.  The authors also explore the differences in how open-source and closed-source models respond to different serialization and prompting approaches. Finally, they propose a reinforcement learning-inspired framework for adaptively selecting optimal factors that influence LLMs' reasoning capabilities.

**Rigorous and Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Benchmark:** GRAPHOMNI addresses a significant gap in the LLM literature by providing a much-needed comprehensive benchmark for graph-theoretic reasoning. It moves beyond isolated studies of prompting strategies or serialization formats, offering a more holistic and systematic evaluation.
    *   **Extendability and Flexibility:** The design of GRAPHOMNI as a modular and extendable framework is a significant strength. The ability to easily add new graph generators, serialization methods, and prompt strategies ensures that the benchmark remains relevant and adaptable as the field evolves. This is a critical feature, as both LLM capabilities and graph representation techniques are rapidly advancing.
    *   **Systematic Evaluation and Insights:** The paper provides insightful empirical observations.  The findings highlight the importance of considering the interactions between graph type, serialization format, and prompting schemes, as well as the model-specific sensitivities to these dimensions. The identification of common error patterns provides valuable diagnostic information for future research.
    *   **Practical Application:** The reinforcement learning-inspired framework for adaptive prompt and serialization selection is a practical contribution.  It shows promise for improving LLM performance by dynamically tailoring the input based on the specific task and model characteristics.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Synthetic Graphs:** The benchmark relies solely on synthetic graphs.  While this allows for controlled experimentation, it might not fully capture the complexities and nuances of real-world graph data. The paper doesn't thoroughly justify the selection of the specific graph types, and the potential for bias in the evaluation due to this choice is a valid concern.
    *   **Limited Depth on RL-based Approach:** While the RL framework is interesting, its development and evaluation seem relatively shallow. The paper provides limited details on the RL implementation and presents a relatively small set of experiments.  A more in-depth investigation of the RL approach, including different RL algorithms, reward structures, and optimization strategies, would strengthen the contribution. Also, it would be interesting to see if the chosen factors could be reduced by applying some feature engineering or feature selection techniques that could speed up the RL convergence.
    *   **Modest Model Improvement:** The performance gains from the proposed RL technique, though notable, don't represent a massive leap in accuracy. This suggests that the factors selected might not be the *most* crucial. Furthermore, the framework has only been tested on relatively simple graph problems which makes it questionable for more complex reasoning problems.
    *   **Lack of Real-World Tasks:** The paper lacks demonstrations of GRAPHOMNI on real-world graph-based tasks, such as knowledge graph reasoning, social network analysis, or biological network analysis. This significantly limits the practical relevance and impact of the benchmark.

*   **Novelty and Significance:** The benchmark is novel in its comprehensive consideration of multiple factors influencing LLM performance on graph tasks. The framework's extendability and the insights into model-specific sensitivities are valuable. However, the reliance on synthetic data and the limited depth of the RL-based adaptive method somewhat reduce the impact. More significant findings will become evident as the benchmark is evaluated further.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:**
GRAPHOMNI represents a valuable contribution to the field by providing a much-needed comprehensive benchmark for evaluating LLMs on graph-theoretic tasks. Its key strengths lie in its systematic approach, extendable design, and insightful empirical findings. However, several weaknesses, including reliance on synthetic graphs, limited depth of the RL approach, and lack of real-world task demonstrations, prevent a higher score.  The paper provides a solid foundation for future research in this area, but the benchmark's ultimate impact will depend on its adoption by the community and its ability to drive meaningful advancements in LLM-based graph reasoning. The relatively modest performance gains achieved with the RL technique suggest that more work is needed to identify and effectively leverage the most impactful factors influencing LLM reasoning capabilities on graphs.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Enhancing the Geometric Problem-Solving Ability of Multimodal LLMs via Symbolic-Neural Integration
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.12773v1)
- **Authors**: Yicheng Pan, Zhenrong Zhang, Pengfei Hu, Jiefeng Ma, Jun Du, Jianshu Zhang, Quan Liu, Jianqing Gao, Feng Ma
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have achieved remarkable progress in general domains and demonstrated promise in multimodal mathematical reasoning. However, applying MLLMs to geometry problem solving (GPS) remains challenging due to lack of accurate step-by-step solution data and severe hallucinations during reasoning. In this paper, we propose GeoGen, a pipeline that can automatically generates step-wise reasoning paths for geometry diagrams. By leveraging the precise symbolic reasoning, \textbf{GeoGen} produces large-scale, high-quality question-answer pairs. To further enhance the logical reasoning ability of MLLMs, we train \textbf{GeoLogic}, a Large Language Model (LLM) using synthetic data generated by GeoGen. Serving as a bridge between natural language and symbolic systems, GeoLogic enables symbolic tools to help verifying MLLM outputs, making the reasoning process more rigorous and alleviating hallucinations. Experimental results show that our approach consistently improves the performance of MLLMs, achieving remarkable results on benchmarks for geometric reasoning tasks. This improvement stems from our integration of the strengths of LLMs and symbolic systems, which enables a more reliable and interpretable approach for the GPS task. Codes are available at https://github.com/ycpNotFound/GeoGen.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of applying Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) to Geometry Problem Solving (GPS). It proposes a novel pipeline called GeoGen to automatically generate step-wise reasoning paths for geometry diagrams. GeoGen leverages precise symbolic reasoning to produce high-quality question-answer pairs, addressing the lack of such data in existing datasets. Additionally, the paper introduces GeoLogic, an LLM trained to translate natural language solutions into formal representations, enabling symbolic verification of MLLM outputs during inference.  This symbolic-neural integration aims to improve the rigor and reliability of the reasoning process, alleviating hallucinations. The authors create two datasets, GeoExpand and GeoSynth, and demonstrate consistent improvements in MLLM performance on geometry benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

*   **GeoGen Pipeline:** The automatic generation of step-by-step reasoning paths using a combination of symbolic reasoning and LLMs is a significant contribution. Existing methods often rely on templates, LLM-generated solutions (which can be unreliable), or manual rule crafting. GeoGen offers a more structured and reliable approach.
*   **GeoLogic:** Using a separate LLM (GeoLogic) as a bridge between natural language and formal symbolic representations is a clever way to leverage symbolic systems as tools within the MLLM framework. This facilitates verification and adds a layer of interpretability.
*   **Datasets:** The creation and release of the GeoExpand and GeoSynth datasets contribute significantly to the field. The scarcity of high-quality, step-by-step solution data has been a major bottleneck for training MLLMs for GPS.

**Significance:**

*   **Improved Performance on GPS:** The experimental results demonstrate a clear and consistent improvement in MLLM performance on geometry benchmarks. This validates the effectiveness of the proposed approach.
*   **Addressing Hallucinations:** The symbolic verification process helps to reduce hallucinations, a common problem in MLLMs, by ensuring that the reasoning process aligns with geometric principles.
*   **Interpretable Reasoning:** By integrating symbolic reasoning, the paper moves towards more interpretable and reliable MLLM-based GPS systems.

**Strengths:**

*   The paper is well-motivated, clearly explaining the challenges of applying MLLMs to GPS and the limitations of existing approaches.
*   The proposed GeoGen pipeline is innovative and well-designed, combining the strengths of symbolic reasoning and LLMs.
*   The creation of GeoExpand and GeoSynth significantly addresses the data scarcity issue in GPS.
*   The experimental results are comprehensive, evaluating the approach across multiple MLLM architectures and datasets.
*   The case study provides a concrete example of how the proposed methods improve the reasoning process.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limitations of Symbolic Verification:**  The paper acknowledges that the symbolic verification process only checks necessary conditions and not sufficiency. This means that the verification process, while beneficial, is not foolproof. This is a limitation that future work should aim to address.
*   **Reliance on External LLM (for template solution transcription):** Although Qwen2.5 is used for translating template solutions from formal language, there is still the possibility of factual errors and hallucination.
*   **Complexity of GeoGen Pipeline:**  The GeoGen pipeline is quite complex, involving multiple components (Plotter, Target Finder, LLM for transcription, symbolic reasoning system). While this complexity allows for precise and high-quality data generation, it may also make the system difficult to implement and maintain.
*   **Scope of Geometric Knowledge:**  The reliance on a fixed set of geometric predicates and theorems limits the scope of problems GeoGen can generate.  Expanding this knowledge base would be a useful direction for future work.
*   **Ablation studies:** While the ablation studies on data composition are interesting, ablation studies of components of the method are missing.

**Potential Influence:**

*   The paper's approach of integrating symbolic reasoning and LLMs could influence future research in multimodal reasoning and other domains.
*   The GeoExpand and GeoSynth datasets are likely to become valuable resources for training and evaluating MLLMs for GPS.
*   The work encourages further exploration of how symbolic tools can be used to enhance the reliability and interpretability of MLLMs.

**Score:** 7.5

**Justification:**

The paper presents a significant contribution to the field of MLLMs for geometry problem solving. The novelty lies in the innovative GeoGen pipeline, the use of GeoLogic for symbolic verification, and the creation of new datasets. The results are convincing, showing improved performance and reduced hallucinations. However, the limitations of the symbolic verification process, the complexity of the pipeline, and reliance on specific symbolic tool limits the significance of the work. The need to expand geometric predicate and theorem coverage of the tool is also important. It represents a substantial step forward, with clear directions for future work, warranting the 7.5 score.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### EarthGPT-X: Enabling MLLMs to Flexibly and Comprehensively Understand Multi-Source Remote Sensing Imagery
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.12795v1)
- **Authors**: Wei Zhang, Miaoxin Cai, Yaqian Ning, Tong Zhang, Yin Zhuang, He Chen, Jun Li, Xuerui Mao
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in the visual-language area have developed natural multi-modal large language models (MLLMs) for spatial reasoning through visual prompting. However, due to remote sensing (RS) imagery containing abundant geospatial information that differs from natural images, it is challenging to effectively adapt natural spatial models to the RS domain. Moreover, current RS MLLMs are limited in overly narrow interpretation levels and interaction manner, hindering their applicability in real-world scenarios. To address those challenges, a spatial MLLM named EarthGPT-X is proposed, enabling a comprehensive understanding of multi-source RS imagery, such as optical, synthetic aperture radar (SAR), and infrared. EarthGPT-X offers zoom-in and zoom-out insight, and possesses flexible multi-grained interactive abilities. Moreover, EarthGPT-X unifies two types of critical spatial tasks (i.e., referring and grounding) into a visual prompting framework. To achieve these versatile capabilities, several key strategies are developed. The first is the multi-modal content integration method, which enhances the interplay between images, visual prompts, and text instructions. Subsequently, a cross-domain one-stage fusion training strategy is proposed, utilizing the large language model (LLM) as a unified interface for multi-source multi-task learning. Furthermore, by incorporating a pixel perception module, the referring and grounding tasks are seamlessly unified within a single framework. In addition, the experiments conducted demonstrate the superiority of the proposed EarthGPT-X in multi-grained tasks and its impressive flexibility in multi-modal interaction, revealing significant advancements of MLLM in the RS field.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "EarthGPT-X: Enabling MLLMs to Flexibly and Comprehensively Understand Multi-Source Remote Sensing Imagery" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces EarthGPT-X, a novel spatial Multi-Modal Large Language Model (MLLM) designed to comprehensively understand multi-source remote sensing (RS) imagery (optical, SAR, infrared). It addresses limitations of existing RS MLLMs, which often lack spatial perception, rely heavily on text instructions, and offer limited interaction capabilities. EarthGPT-X achieves flexible multi-grained interactive abilities, offering zoom-in and zoom-out insights, by unifying referring and grounding tasks within a visual prompting framework. Key strategies include: a multi-modal content integration method, a cross-domain one-stage fusion training strategy utilizing a Large Language Model (LLM) as a unified interface, and a pixel perception module.  Experiments demonstrate the superiority of EarthGPT-X in multi-grained tasks and multi-modal interaction. The paper also presents M-RSVP, a new multi-source visual prompting dataset for training EarthGPT-X.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a significant step forward for MLLMs in the remote sensing domain, addressing crucial limitations in existing models. The core novelty lies in combining several elements: 1) the unification of referring and grounding tasks within a visual prompting framework for RS; 2) the comprehensive multi-source data handling (optical, SAR, infrared) with a specifically tailored training strategy; 3) the multi-grained interaction capabilities, enabling both detailed and overview-level analyses; and 4) The introduction of the M-RSVP dataset. While individual components (visual prompting, multi-source data fusion) have been explored in other contexts, their combination within the RS domain and their specific adaptation to RS imagery characteristics is genuinely novel. Compared to EarthGPT, this paper adds new spatial-aware visual prompting that enhances the flexibility of human-AI interactions. This addresses limitations in EarthGPT, as well as GeoChat.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical need in RS image analysis: enabling more intuitive and comprehensive interaction with complex data. EarthGPT-X's capabilities have potential impact for various real-world applications, including environmental monitoring, disaster response, and urban planning. Its ability to process data from multiple sensors and understand spatial relationships could significantly improve decision-making in these fields. The M-RSVP dataset is also valuable as a new resource for the RS community. However, it is important to note that the dataset is a re-annotation of a dataset, which decreases the significance.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem statement and well-defined objectives.
    *   A well-designed architecture incorporating multi-modal data.
    *   Thorough experimental evaluation using various metrics.
    *   Demonstrated superior performance compared to existing models.
    *   Addresses limitations of existing RS MLLMs.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The experimental section mainly emphasizes comparison with existing models on existing datasets (i.e., DIOR-RSVG and OPT-RSVG), this may not fully highlight new benefits from multi-sourced data. More analysis on the effectiveness of learning from multi-sourced data is needed.
    *   The cross-domain training strategy is mentioned as one of the contributions. More ablation results on how cross-domain training improved the performance on SAR and infrared data would strengthen this statement.
    *   There is no discussion of the computational cost and inference efficiency.

*   **Justification of the Score:** Considering both the strengths and weaknesses, the paper makes a substantial contribution to MLLMs in RS. The design of EarthGPT-X provides a more flexible and comprehensive approach to understanding multi-source RS imagery compared to previous models. This represents a noticeable advancement within the RS domain. However, there are points for improvement in the experimental evaluation and the discussion of computational resources.

**Score: 7.5**
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Assesing LLMs in Art Contexts: Critique Generation and Theory of Mind Evaluation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.12805v1)
- **Authors**: Takaya Arita, Wenxian Zheng, Reiji Suzuki, Fuminori Akiba
- **Abstract**: This study explored how large language models (LLMs) perform in two areas related to art: writing critiques of artworks and reasoning about mental states (Theory of Mind, or ToM) in art-related situations. For the critique generation part, we built a system that combines Noel Carroll's evaluative framework with a broad selection of art criticism theories. The model was prompted to first write a full-length critique and then shorter, more coherent versions using a step-by-step prompting process. These AI-generated critiques were then compared with those written by human experts in a Turing test-style evaluation. In many cases, human subjects had difficulty telling which was which, and the results suggest that LLMs can produce critiques that are not only plausible in style but also rich in interpretation, as long as they are carefully guided. In the second part, we introduced new simple ToM tasks based on situations involving interpretation, emotion, and moral tension, which can appear in the context of art. These go beyond standard false-belief tests and allow for more complex, socially embedded forms of reasoning. We tested 41 recent LLMs and found that their performance varied across tasks and models. In particular, tasks that involved affective or ambiguous situations tended to reveal clearer differences. Taken together, these results help clarify how LLMs respond to complex interpretative challenges, revealing both their cognitive limitations and potential. While our findings do not directly contradict the so-called Generative AI Paradox--the idea that LLMs can produce expert-like output without genuine understanding--they suggest that, depending on how LLMs are instructed, such as through carefully designed prompts, these models may begin to show behaviors that resemble understanding more closely than we might assume.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Assessing LLMs in Art Contexts: Critique Generation and Theory of Mind Evaluation" explores the capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) in two art-related domains: generating art critiques and demonstrating Theory of Mind (ToM) in art-related scenarios.

For critique generation, the authors built a system that combines Noël Carroll's evaluative framework with various art criticism theories.  They use a step-by-step prompting process to generate full-length and condensed critiques, then conduct a Turing test, comparing AI-generated critiques with human-written ones. The results suggest LLMs can generate plausible and rich critiques when carefully prompted.

In the ToM evaluation, they introduce novel tasks based on interpretation, emotion, and moral tension in art contexts. They tested 41 LLMs on these tasks, revealing varying performance across models, particularly in affective and ambiguous situations. The findings clarify how LLMs respond to complex interpretive challenges, revealing both cognitive limitations and potential. The authors argue that depending on how LLMs are instructed, they can begin to show behaviors that resemble understanding more closely than we might assume.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novelty:** The paper is innovative in its dual approach, combining critique generation with ToM evaluation in the art domain.  While individual elements may exist in other research, the integration is unique. The newly introduced ToM tasks are well-defined and adapted to the specific nuances of art interpretation.
    *   **Rigorous Methodology:**  The Turing test is well-designed and controls for potential biases. The careful curation of data, especially the rules R1-R5 for a fair comparison between AI and human critiques, adds to the rigor. The ToM tasks are systematically tested across a significant number of LLMs.
    *   **Theoretical Foundation:** Grounding the critique generation in Noël Carroll's framework provides a strong theoretical base. Categorizing the proposed ToM tasks with "Abilities in Theory of Mind Space" (ATOMS) enhances the study's grounding.
    *   **Practical Implications:** The paper's findings are relevant for AI art education, curating, and exhibition design.  The LLM's abilities also highlight the opportunities and challenges of using AI in creative fields and understanding how it affects human creativity.
    *   **Clear presentation:** The paper is well written, with the methods, results and figures providing clear understanding of the experiments and findings.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Human Evaluation:**  The Turing test, while well-designed, had only 60 participants. A larger and more diverse group would strengthen the findings' generalizability.
    *   **Subjectivity of ToM Tasks:** The "correct" answers to the ToM tasks are still based on human interpretation, which may introduce bias even within the structured questions. The reliance on what are considered "expected answers" to questions that probe individual and social-norm based emotional reasoning lacks a certain degree of robustness, and may result in unintended bias.
    *   **Limited LLM Diversity:** All LLMs that were tested were accessed via the ChatHub interface at a specific point in time (March 2024), resulting in limitations regarding the diversity of LLMs that could be included. Additional models were later added, but could have been included in the initial evaluation stage.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper pushes the boundaries of LLM research beyond simple content generation by exploring higher-level cognitive functions like understanding, interpretation, and emotional reasoning in complex and subjective art contexts.
    *   It sheds light on the "Generative AI Paradox," suggesting LLMs may exhibit behaviors that resemble understanding more closely than initially thought. This has broader implications for AI ethics and the future of human-AI collaboration.
    *   The novel ToM tasks could serve as a benchmark for future research in evaluating LLMs' ability to reason about human mental states in culturally and emotionally rich scenarios.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper demonstrates moderate novelty and significant potential for impact. While the individual techniques are not entirely new, the synthesis of critique generation with ToM evaluation, the well-designed methodology, and the novel ToM tasks justify a high-end moderate score. The findings are interesting and lead to a number of future research avenues, such as further exploration and development of prompt strategies that are more reflective of human cognitive processes, expanding the study to be more cross-cultural, and further exploration of bias within LLMs. Given the number of limitations that were acknowledged by the authors, I would conclude that further investigation is required to truly understand LLMs and their capabilities.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Saliency-Aware Diffusion Reconstruction for Effective Invisible Watermark Removal
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.12809v1)
- **Authors**: Inzamamul Alam, Md Tanvir Islam, Simon S. Woo
- **Abstract**: As digital content becomes increasingly ubiquitous, the need for robust watermark removal techniques has grown due to the inadequacy of existing embedding techniques, which lack robustness. This paper introduces a novel Saliency-Aware Diffusion Reconstruction (SADRE) framework for watermark elimination on the web, combining adaptive noise injection, region-specific perturbations, and advanced diffusion-based reconstruction. SADRE disrupts embedded watermarks by injecting targeted noise into latent representations guided by saliency masks although preserving essential image features. A reverse diffusion process ensures high-fidelity image restoration, leveraging adaptive noise levels determined by watermark strength. Our framework is theoretically grounded with stability guarantees and achieves robust watermark removal across diverse scenarios. Empirical evaluations on state-of-the-art (SOTA) watermarking techniques demonstrate SADRE's superiority in balancing watermark disruption and image quality. SADRE sets a new benchmark for watermark elimination, offering a flexible and reliable solution for real-world web content. Code is available on~\href{https://github.com/inzamamulDU/SADRE}{\textbf{https://github.com/inzamamulDU/SADRE}}.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Saliency-Aware Diffusion Reconstruction (SADRE), a novel framework for removing invisible watermarks from images. SADRE combines adaptive noise injection, region-specific perturbations based on a saliency mask, and diffusion-based reconstruction. The core idea is to inject targeted noise into the latent representation of the watermarked image, guided by a saliency mask that identifies regions most likely affected by the watermark. A reverse diffusion process then restores the image while minimizing artifacts. The paper theoretically grounds SADRE with stability guarantees and demonstrates its effectiveness across various watermarking techniques, showing superior performance in balancing watermark removal and image quality compared to existing methods. The authors provide code for their work, increasing its accessibility and potential for replication and extension by others.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the integration of saliency-aware noise injection with diffusion models for watermark removal. While individual components (diffusion models, saliency detection) are not novel, the combination is unique and addresses a significant limitation in existing methods: the trade-off between watermark removal and image quality. The adaptive noise injection based on estimated watermark strength is another novel element.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper is substantial. Watermark removal is a practical problem with implications for copyright protection and digital asset management. The existing watermarking techniques are often breakable, and the watermarks used have limited robustness. The proposed method offers a way to remove watermarks with high fidelity to the original image. The theoretical foundations provide a layer of robustness that heuristic-based approaches often lack. The provision of code further enhances the practical impact.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Strong theoretical grounding and stability guarantees.
    *   Empirically validated performance improvement across multiple watermarking methods.
    *   Adaptive noise injection strategy minimizes collateral damage to the image.
    *   The code is released, which ensures reproducibility and promotes further research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The saliency detection component could be a bottleneck. If the saliency map is inaccurate, the noise injection could be less effective or introduce unwanted artifacts. The paper may benefit from a discussion about the robustness of the method to different types of saliency detectors and potential failure modes.
    *   While the paper discusses adaptive noise level selection, the parameter tuning process might require careful adjustment for different watermarking schemes and image types, which could limit ease-of-use in real-world scenarios. More insights into the practical parameter selection would strengthen the paper.
    *   The computational complexity of diffusion models is generally high. The paper would benefit from discussing the computational cost of SADRE and possible optimizations.

*   **Rigorousness:** The paper demonstrates strong results by quantitatively evaluating performance using appropriate metrics (PSNR, SSIM, Wp, BRA). The comparison to several state-of-the-art watermarking techniques adds credibility. The authors explicitly define the threat model, assumptions, and address limitations.

*   **Potential Impact:** SADRE has the potential to significantly influence the field of watermark removal by providing a more robust and effective approach. It can also inform the development of more resilient watermarking techniques designed to resist such attacks. The saliency-aware approach could be generalized to other image editing and restoration tasks.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:** SADRE represents a significant advancement in watermark removal by effectively balancing watermark disruption with image quality preservation. The combination of saliency awareness and diffusion-based reconstruction is innovative and supported by solid theoretical foundations and extensive empirical validation. While the saliency detection component and computational cost could be improved, SADRE offers a compelling solution with substantial practical and theoretical contributions to the field.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Image-Editing Specialists: An RLAIF Approach for Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.12833v1)
- **Authors**: Elior Benarous, Yilun Du, Heng Yang
- **Abstract**: We present a novel approach to training specialized instruction-based image-editing diffusion models, addressing key challenges in structural preservation with input images and semantic alignment with user prompts. We introduce an online reinforcement learning framework that aligns the diffusion model with human preferences without relying on extensive human annotations or curating a large dataset. Our method significantly improves the realism and alignment with instructions in two ways. First, the proposed models achieve precise and structurally coherent modifications in complex scenes while maintaining high fidelity in instruction-irrelevant areas. Second, they capture fine nuances in the desired edit by leveraging a visual prompt, enabling detailed control over visual edits without lengthy textual prompts. This approach simplifies users' efforts to achieve highly specific edits, requiring only 5 reference images depicting a certain concept for training. Experimental results demonstrate that our models can perform intricate edits in complex scenes, after just 10 training steps. Finally, we showcase the versatility of our method by applying it to robotics, where enhancing the visual realism of simulated environments through targeted sim-to-real image edits improves their utility as proxies for real-world settings.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel approach for training instruction-based image editing diffusion models.  It focuses on addressing challenges related to structural preservation and semantic alignment using Reinforcement Learning from AI Feedback (RLAIF). Instead of relying on extensive human annotations for training, the method leverages AI-generated feedback to enforce both structural coherence with the input image and semantic consistency with the user's text instructions and visual prompts.  The method fine-tunes diffusion models using a self-play framework and demonstrates superior performance compared to existing state-of-the-art approaches in terms of precision, realism, and adherence to instructions. It also illustrates how this method can be applied to enhance the visual realism of simulated environments in robotics.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents several novel aspects:
    *   The use of RLAIF for instruction-based image editing, eliminating the need for costly and potentially inconsistent human annotations. This departs from prior works relying on human feedback (RLHF) or curated synthetic datasets.
    *   The combination of textual instructions and *visual* prompts, offering finer-grained control over edits by leveraging the strengths of both modalities, and encoding human intuition that are hard to articulate through text.
    *   The self-play framework, where the diffusion model's own generated samples are used for training, allowing the model to learn from its mistakes and adapt to user expectations without being restricted by a fixed, pre-collected dataset. This contrasts with large-scale finetuning that rely on generated data from external oracles and limits the method's generalizability.
    *   The decoupling of the objective function into structural and semantic alignment components allows for more targeted learning and performance improvements.
    *   The specific architecture modifications to better incorporate the multiple conditioning signals (text, visual prompts, and input image).
*   **Significance:**
    *   The method addresses a key limitation of existing instruction-based image editing models: the difficulty in achieving both precise edits and high structural coherence, especially in complex scenes. By focusing on these two criteria, the paper contributes to making such models more practically useful.
    *   The ability to enhance simulated environments for robotics with more realistic edits has significant potential for improving the reliability of simulation-based training and policy evaluation, which is important because it allows for safer and cheaper experiments, without risks of damaging equipment.
    *   The reliance on AI-generated feedback makes the training process more scalable and adaptable than methods relying on human annotations.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing methods and the challenges it aims to address.
    *   The proposed approach is well-motivated and technically sound.
    *   The experimental results demonstrate a clear improvement over state-of-the-art baselines, both qualitatively and quantitatively.
    *   The ablation studies provide insights into the importance of the different components of the proposed method.
    *   The application to robotics demonstrates the practical utility of the approach.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper could benefit from a more detailed discussion of the limitations of the proposed method, e.g., potential biases inherited from the AI models used for feedback generation or the types of edits that are most challenging.
    *   The computational cost of RLAIF, while reducing the need for human annotations, can be substantial. A comparison of the computational requirements compared to RLHF would be helpful.
    *   The choice of hyperparameters, particularly the guidance scales, seems to require some tuning. A more systematic approach to hyperparameter selection could improve the reproducibility and generalizability of the results.
    *   The claim of being able to modify the cabinet's "material" might be an overstatement, given that it's only modifying the visual appearance.
*   **Potential Impact:**
    *   The paper has the potential to influence the development of more practical and reliable instruction-based image editing tools.
    *   The application to robotics could contribute to more effective simulation-based training and policy evaluation.
    *   The use of RLAIF could inspire further research on using AI-generated feedback for training generative models.

**Score:** 7.5

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel and technically sound approach to instruction-based image editing that addresses significant limitations of existing methods. The use of RLAIF and visual prompts is a valuable contribution. The results clearly demonstrate the superiority of the proposed method, and the application to robotics highlights its practical utility.

However, the method is not without limitations. Further clarification on the precise extent of the edits possible, computational cost, potential biases, and hyperparameter tuning would improve the paper further. While the contribution is significant, it's not a *breakthrough* that fundamentally transforms the field; rather, it's a solid, incremental advance with good potential impact. The score reflects that it represents a valuable contribution that is not yet a "game changer".

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### DashChat: Interactive Authoring of Industrial Dashboard Design Prototypes through Conversation with LLM-Powered Agents
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.12865v1)
- **Authors**: S. Shen, Z. Lin, W. Liu, C. Xin, W. Dai, S. Chen, X. Wen, X. Lan
- **Abstract**: Industrial dashboards, commonly deployed by organizations such as enterprises and governments, are increasingly crucial in data communication and decision-making support across various domains. Designing an industrial dashboard prototype is particularly challenging due to its visual complexity, which can include data visualization, layout configuration, embellishments, and animations. Additionally, in real-world industrial settings, designers often encounter numerous constraints. For instance, when companies negotiate collaborations with clients and determine design plans, they typically need to demo design prototypes and iterate on them based on mock data quickly. Such a task is very common and crucial during the ideation stage, as it not only helps save developmental costs but also avoids data-related issues such as lengthy data handover periods. However, existing authoring tools of dashboards are mostly not tailored to such prototyping needs, and motivated by these gaps, we propose DashChat, an interactive system that leverages large language models (LLMs) to generate industrial dashboard design prototypes from natural language. We collaborated closely with designers from the industry and derived the requirements based on their practical experience. First, by analyzing 114 high-quality industrial dashboards, we summarized their common design patterns and inject the identified ones into LLMs as reference. Next, we built a multi-agent pipeline powered by LLMs to understand textual requirements from users and generate practical, aesthetic prototypes. Besides, functionally distinct, parallel-operating agents are created to enable efficient generation. Then, we developed a user-friendly interface that supports text-based interaction for generating and modifying prototypes. Two user studies demonstrated that our system is both effective and efficient in supporting design prototyping.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DashChat, an interactive system that assists designers in creating industrial dashboard prototypes using natural language and large language models (LLMs). It addresses the challenge of dashboard design, which is complex due to visual elements and real-world constraints (like data access limitations). The approach involves analyzing existing industrial dashboards to identify common design patterns, incorporating these patterns into LLMs, and building a multi-agent pipeline to translate textual requirements into prototype designs. A user-friendly interface is developed to facilitate text-based interaction for prototype generation and modification. User studies demonstrate the system's effectiveness and efficiency in supporting design prototyping. The key contributions include an analysis of industrial dashboard design space, a multi-agent pipeline powered by LLMs, and user studies verifying the system's usability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its specific focus on industrial dashboard *prototyping* using LLMs and a multi-agent approach. While LLMs have been used in visualization and dashboard creation before (as the related work section shows), DashChat's tailored approach to industrial dashboards, the emphasis on *prototyping* rather than final dashboards, and the explicit incorporation of a design space derived from empirical analysis makes it a somewhat distinct contribution. Existing tools and research tend to focus on data-driven chart recommendations or design ideation in general, rather than text-driven prototype generation of dashboards with specific industrial use-cases in mind. The identified design space from 114 dashboards is a valuable contribution, although its depth and generalizability could be questioned (see below).

*   **Significance:** The significance is moderate. The system tackles a real-world problem faced by dashboard designers in industry, where rapid prototyping is often needed before data is readily available. The user studies suggest that the system improves efficiency and accelerates the design process. However, the impact is limited by the scope: it's primarily a prototyping tool, not a full-fledged dashboard creation suite. The paper convincingly demonstrates how LLMs can significantly shorten the feedback loop between business users and designers by enabling users to refine their high-level visions quickly. It could potentially impact the initial stages of dashboard development workflows.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Real-world relevance:** The work is directly motivated by industrial practice and is grounded in the authors' experience at Alibaba Cloud's DataV.
    *   **Empirical foundation:** The design space analysis provides a solid foundation for the system's design and improves the quality of generated prototypes.
    *   **Multi-agent architecture:** The use of parallel operating LLM agents contributes to efficient generation of prototypes.
    *   **User studies:**  User studies are employed to evaluate both prototype quality and system usability.
    *   **Comprehensive related work:** The paper thoroughly discusses relevant prior research.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Design Space Analysis:** While the study of 114 dashboards is a good start, the depth of analysis and the generalizability of the identified design space patterns may be limited. A larger, more diverse dataset of industrial dashboards would have strengthened this aspect of the work.
    *   **LLM Dependency:** The performance of the system heavily relies on the underlying LLMs (Qwen2.5-Max). Advances or limitations in LLM technology will directly impact DashChat's capabilities. The paper offers limited insights into how the system addresses the inherent unreliability and potential biases of LLMs.
    *   **Limited discussion of data quality and lineage during rapid prototyping.** The paper mentions data being simulated, but there's no discussion of how the team is accounting for data provenance and validity, even in the prototype phase. How does this affect downstream work?
    *   **Qualitative data analysis could be enriched.** In addition to the ratings, it is important to provide a detailed qualitative analysis of the answers that were provided in open-ended questions.
    *   **Unclear Generalizability:** While user studies validate the approach for the given participants, the generalizability of these findings to a wider range of designers and industrial contexts remains unclear.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could encourage further research on LLM-powered design tools specifically for industrial applications. It could also inspire the development of more sophisticated design space models for dashboards and other complex visualizations. The idea of using LLMs for rapid prototyping in data visualization could become a more established practice.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a solid contribution by addressing a practical problem in industrial dashboard design. The approach is reasonably novel, and the user studies provide evidence of its effectiveness. However, the limited scope (prototyping only), the reliance on LLMs, and limitations in the design space analysis prevent it from being a truly groundbreaking contribution. It builds on existing work in LLM-powered visualization but applies it to a focused area. While the system offers clear benefits, it doesn't represent a paradigm shift in dashboard design or significantly alter the way professional designers typically work.
