### HiScene: Creating Hierarchical 3D Scenes with Isometric View Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.13072v1)
- **Authors**: Wenqi Dong, Bangbang Yang, Zesong Yang, Yuan Li, Tao Hu, Hujun Bao, Yuewen Ma, Zhaopeng Cui
- **Abstract**: Scene-level 3D generation represents a critical frontier in multimedia and computer graphics, yet existing approaches either suffer from limited object categories or lack editing flexibility for interactive applications. In this paper, we present HiScene, a novel hierarchical framework that bridges the gap between 2D image generation and 3D object generation and delivers high-fidelity scenes with compositional identities and aesthetic scene content. Our key insight is treating scenes as hierarchical "objects" under isometric views, where a room functions as a complex object that can be further decomposed into manipulatable items. This hierarchical approach enables us to generate 3D content that aligns with 2D representations while maintaining compositional structure. To ensure completeness and spatial alignment of each decomposed instance, we develop a video-diffusion-based amodal completion technique that effectively handles occlusions and shadows between objects, and introduce shape prior injection to ensure spatial coherence within the scene. Experimental results demonstrate that our method produces more natural object arrangements and complete object instances suitable for interactive applications, while maintaining physical plausibility and alignment with user inputs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "HiScene: Creating Hierarchical 3D Scenes with Isometric View Generation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces HiScene, a novel framework for generating compositional 3D scenes from isometric views. The core idea is to treat entire scenes as hierarchical "objects" that can be decomposed into manipulatable items.  HiScene leverages image generation models and video diffusion models to produce high-fidelity 3D scenes with compositional identities and aesthetic scene content. The framework includes a hierarchical scene parsing approach, video-diffusion-based amodal completion to handle occlusions, and a shape prior injection mechanism to ensure spatial coherence.  The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of their approach through quantitative and qualitative evaluations, showcasing improved object arrangements, completeness, and spatial alignment compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper presents a well-integrated pipeline, however, each of its components is inspired from existing literature. The main contribution lies in the combination of several ideas.

*   **Hierarchical Scene Representation:** While the concept of hierarchical scene representation is not entirely new, the use of isometric views as a mechanism to bridge 2D image generation and 3D object generation is a novel perspective. It allows leveraging the strengths of 2D generative models to guide 3D scene creation.
*   **Video-Diffusion-Based Amodal Completion:** Reframing amodal completion as a video generation task to handle occlusions and shadows is a clever idea. The dedicated dataset curation effort to train the video diffusion model is also noteworthy. Although amodal completion and shadow removal exist, the combined approach within a video diffusion framework in the context of 3D scene generation appears to be a novel contribution.
*   **Shape Prior Injection:** The technique of using view-aligned generation methods to extract shape priors and inject them into the native 3D generation process is a valuable contribution for maintaining spatial alignment. This leverages existing view-aligned methods and incorporates them in an approach to reduce geometric ambiguities during refinement.
*   **Integrated Pipeline:** The most novel aspect of the paper lies in the combination of these different techniques into a coherent framework for 3D scene generation.

**Significance:**

*   **Improved Scene Generation:** The results demonstrate that HiScene can generate more realistic, diverse, and compositionally sound 3D scenes compared to existing approaches. The ability to manipulate individual objects within the scene significantly enhances its utility for interactive applications and data curation for robotics.
*   **Bridging 2D and 3D Generation:** The approach offers a promising way to leverage the advancements in 2D generative models for the creation of complex 3D content. This could open up new avenues for research and development in scene understanding, virtual reality, and game development.
*   **Potential Impact:** The framework contributes to the accessibility of 3D scene creation, enabling users to generate detailed and interactive 3D environments from simple text prompts or isometric view images. This ease of use can further democratize 3D content creation and promote creativity.
*   **Limitations:** The paper discusses textures with baked lighting as a limitation, however, more should be discussed regarding the limitations with rendering and performance on different platforms. Additionally, although a lot is mentioned about the ability to manipulate scenes in real time, nothing is included about how this will be handled, or what the approximate FPS is.

**Justification:**
HiScene offers a novel approach to 3D scene generation by innovatively combining hierarchical representations, video diffusion for amodal completion, and shape prior injection, resulting in superior scene composition and interactivity. While the approach relies on existing components, the integrated pipeline enhances 3D content creation, making it more accessible and versatile. However, further discussion is needed in regards to real-time performance, and implementation.
